[X] Prompt 0007 | 2026-02-16T02:53:26.715Z | verified
You are working inside the existing CoverMeNow ONE custom WordPress plugin.

Implement Prompt 25: Secure encrypted storage + masking for candidate bank details.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Candidate bank details are sensitive.
- Must encrypt at rest.
- Must never display full values after save.
- Must not log sensitive data.
- Must support migration from existing plain records created in Prompt 24.

GOAL
Harden cmn_candidate_bank_details storage by encrypting sort code and account number, while retaining masked display.

A) DB SCHEMA UPDATE
Alter cmn_candidate_bank_details:

Replace:
- sort_code VARCHAR(8)
- account_number VARCHAR(8)

With:
- sort_code_enc VARBINARY(255) NOT NULL
- account_number_enc VARBINARY(255) NOT NULL

Add:
- sort_code_last2 VARCHAR(2) NOT NULL
- account_number_last4 VARCHAR(4) NOT NULL
- enc_version TINYINT DEFAULT 1

Keep:
- account_name as plain text (not sensitive).
- created_at / updated_at.

B) ENCRYPTION IMPLEMENTATION
Implement helper:
cmn_encrypt_secret($plaintext)
cmn_decrypt_secret($ciphertext)

Use:
- openssl_encrypt with AES-256-GCM
- Key loaded from wp-config.php constant or env:
  CMN_BANK_ENC_KEY (32 bytes base64)
- Store nonce + tag with ciphertext safely (e.g. JSON blob or concat format).

IMPORTANT:
- Fail closed if key missing (do not save; show error).

C) MIGRATION
Write one-time migration function:
cmn_migrate_bank_details_plain_to_encrypted()

Logic:
- Find rows that still have sort_code/account_number columns or legacy data.
- Encrypt values.
- Store last2/last4.
- Remove legacy columns after migration (or keep temporarily but stop reading).

D) UI DISPLAY UPDATE
Update Finance page masked preview to use:
- sort_code_last2 as **-**-** format:
  display as “**-**-XX” is too masked; better:
  display “12-34-56” using last2 is impossible.
  Therefore store sort_code_last4 as well:
    Add sort_code_last4 VARCHAR(4) NOT NULL
  and display: “**-**-3456” or store all 6 in masked form.

Preferred approach:
- Store sort_code_masked VARCHAR(8) NOT NULL formatted "12-34-56"
- Store account_number_last4

So final fields should include:
- sort_code_masked VARCHAR(8) NOT NULL
- account_number_last4 VARCHAR(4) NOT NULL

Never decrypt for display.

E) ACCESS CONTROL
- Candidate can only update their own bank details.
- Staff/admin MUST NOT be able to view raw details (even decrypted).
- Only show masked fields.

F) AUDIT
Audit event remains:
module='finance'
event_type='bank_details_updated'
metadata must never include bank fields.

G) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
- Saving bank details fails if key missing.
- Data stored encrypted.
- UI displays masked values only.
- Migration converts existing records safely.
- No logs or audit rows contain bank values.

OUTPUT
- Provide code changes only with file locations.

Implement Prompt 27: Payroll period engine for weekly pay (Friday–Thursday), paid on Friday.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Must be timezone-safe (use WP timezone).
- Must support historic runs.
- No payout calculations yet (Prompt 29).
- Must not require invoices.
- Must be auditable.

GOAL
Create a clean set of helpers that define the “current payroll period”, “previous period”, and period bounds for any date.

A) PERIOD HELPERS
Implement:

1) cmn_payroll_get_period_for_date($date): array
Returns:
[
  'period_start' => Y-m-d (Friday),
  'period_end' => Y-m-d (Thursday),
  'pay_date' => Y-m-d (Friday immediately after period_end),
  'label' => 'Fri DD Mon – Thu DD Mon'
]

Rules:
- If date is Friday, that is the start of a new period.
- Period end is the following Thursday.
- Pay date is the Friday after the Thursday end.

2) cmn_payroll_get_current_period(): array
Uses “today” in WP timezone.

3) cmn_payroll_get_previous_period(): array

B) PERIOD ID
Create stable identifier:
cmn_payroll_period_id($period_start, $period_end): string
Example: "2026-02-13__2026-02-19"

C) AUDIT
Log only when staff views payroll period info (in staff payroll page render):
module='payroll'
event_type='payroll_period_resolved'
metadata={period_start,period_end,pay_date}

D) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
- Given a Monday date, returns previous Friday start and upcoming Thursday end.
- Given a Friday date, returns that same Friday as start.
- Period ID stable.
- Pay date always Friday.

[X] Prompt 0009 | 2026-02-16T03:09:26.309Z | verified
You are working inside the existing CoverMeNow ONE custom WordPress plugin.

Implement Prompt 28: Payroll auto-lock job that runs every Thursday at 17:00 (WP timezone) and auto-approves outstanding candidate bookings in the current pay period (Fri–Thu) for payment purposes.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Must NOT change School Partner confirmation status.
- Must NOT require invoices.
- Must NOT alter school-facing records.
- This is ONLY for candidate pay eligibility.
- Must be idempotent and auditable.

GOAL
At Thu 17:00, lock the payroll period and ensure all worked bookings Fri–Thu are payable even if the school hasn’t confirmed.

A) DATA MODEL
Create table: cmn_payroll_period_locks

Columns:
- id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY
- period_id VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL (from Prompt 27)
- period_start DATE NOT NULL
- period_end DATE NOT NULL
- locked_at DATETIME NOT NULL
- locked_by VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL  ('system','staff')
- created_at DATETIME NOT NULL

Unique key:
- UNIQUE(period_id)

B) PAY-APPROVAL TRACKING (PAY ONLY)
Create table: cmn_candidate_pay_approvals

Columns:
- id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY
- candidate_user_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- booking_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- booking_day_date DATE NOT NULL
- period_id VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL
- approval_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL
   ('approved','held','disputed')
- approved_method VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL
   ('school_confirmed','auto_approved','staff_override')
- approved_at DATETIME NOT NULL
- created_at DATETIME NOT NULL
- updated_at DATETIME NOT NULL

Unique key:
- UNIQUE(candidate_user_id, booking_id, booking_day_date)

C) AUTO-LOCK CRON
Schedule WP-Cron:
- Runs every Thursday 17:00 (WP timezone)

When triggered:
1) Resolve current payroll period (Prompt 27).
2) Insert period lock row if not exists (idempotent).
3) Identify all booking-days in that period that are:
   - status indicates completed/attended OR has attendance mark in system
   - NOT cancelled
4) For each booking-day:
   - If a pay approval row already exists -> skip
   - Else insert approval_status='approved'
     approved_method='auto_approved'
     approved_at=now

IMPORTANT:
- Auto-approval should not require school confirmation.
- If a no-show flag exists for candidate, do NOT approve (leave for dispute/hold).

D) AUDIT EVENTS
- payroll_period_locked (module='payroll', entity_type='period', entity_id=period_id)
- booking_day_auto_approved (module='payroll', entity_type='booking', entity_id=booking_id, metadata={date,candidate_user_id})

E) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
- Cron locks period once.
- Re-running cron does not duplicate locks.
- Approvals created only for eligible booking-days.
- Cancelled booking-days not approved.
- No-show booking-days not auto-approved.
- School partner confirmation system unaffected.

[X] Prompt 0010 | 2026-02-16T03:21:09.364Z | verified
You are working inside the existing CoverMeNow ONE custom WordPress plugin.

Implement Prompt 29: Payout calculation engine and payout run records for the weekly payroll period, using pay approvals created in Prompt 28.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Must use booking rates as source of truth for candidate pay.
- Must only include pay approvals with approval_status='approved'.
- Must respect bank completeness gating (Prompt 26):
   - Missing bank details => status 'hold' in payout run output (still calculated, not paid).
- Must be idempotent for a given period.
- Must not require invoices.

A) DATA TABLE: PAYOUT RUNS
Create table: cmn_payroll_payout_runs

Columns:
- id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY
- period_id VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL
- period_start DATE NOT NULL
- period_end DATE NOT NULL
- generated_at DATETIME NOT NULL
- generated_by VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL ('system','staff')
- total_candidates INT NOT NULL
- total_gross DECIMAL(12,2) NOT NULL
- run_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'generated'
   ('generated','exported','paid','voided')
- created_at DATETIME NOT NULL

Unique key:
- UNIQUE(period_id)

B) DATA TABLE: PAYOUT RUN LINE ITEMS
Create table: cmn_payroll_payout_run_items

Columns:
- id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY
- payout_run_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- candidate_user_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- gross_amount DECIMAL(12,2) NOT NULL
- shifts_count INT NOT NULL
- bank_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL ('complete','missing')
- payout_item_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL ('ready','hold')
- created_at DATETIME NOT NULL

Indexes:
- payout_run_id
- candidate_user_id

C) CALCULATION FUNCTION
Create:
cmn_generate_payout_run_for_period($period_id)

Logic:
1) If payout run already exists for period_id -> return existing run (idempotent).
2) Pull all cmn_candidate_pay_approvals rows for that period_id where approval_status='approved'
3) For each approval row, determine candidate_pay_rate for that booking-day from booking rates source of truth.
4) Sum gross per candidate:
   - shifts_count = number of approved booking-days
   - gross_amount = sum(candidate_pay_rate per day)
5) Determine bank_status using helper (Prompt 26)
6) payout_item_status:
   - 'ready' if bank complete
   - 'hold' if missing
7) Insert payout run + items.
8) Audit events:
   - payout_run_generated
   - payout_run_item_created (aggregate audit allowed, do not spam; one event per run with counts is fine)

D) UI INTEGRATION

1) Staff Payroll page (Prompt 23 shell):
- Replace placeholders with live values for current period if locked:
   - candidates due payment = count of items
   - total gross = sum gross
- Table shows rows with:
   Candidate | Bank status | Shifts credited | Gross | Status (ready/hold)
- Add button:
   “Generate payout run”
   If run exists, show “Payout run generated”.

2) Candidate Finance page (Prompt 22):
- Show:
   Earnings this week (sum of approved days so far within current period)
   Expected payout Friday (if bank missing, show “On hold until bank details added”)
- IMPORTANT: before Thu 17:00 lock, show “Estimate (pending lock)”.

E) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
- Run is generated once per period.
- Gross sums match booking rates.
- Missing bank details => hold.
- Staff payroll shows real totals.
- Candidate finance shows expected payout/hold messaging.

Implement Prompt 30: Dispute and adjustment workflow for payroll, with carry-forward into the next payout run.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Must be auditable.
- Must not mutate historical payout run items once run_status != 'generated'.
- Adjustments should apply in next payout run as “manual adjustment” line.
- Candidates should be able to submit a dispute via Support (wire actual ticketing in Prompt 33/34, but structure here).

A) DATA MODEL: PAYROLL ADJUSTMENTS
Create table: cmn_payroll_adjustments

Columns:
- id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY
- candidate_user_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- period_id VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL
- adjustment_amount DECIMAL(12,2) NOT NULL  (can be negative)
- adjustment_reason VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL
- adjustment_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending'
   ('pending','approved','rejected','applied')
- created_by_user_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- created_at DATETIME NOT NULL
- updated_at DATETIME NOT NULL
- applied_in_period_id VARCHAR(50) NULL

Index:
- candidate_user_id
- period_id
- adjustment_status

B) STAFF WORKFLOW (inside Staff Payroll page)
Add a simple “Adjustments” panel (staff/admin only):
- Create adjustment form:
   Candidate selector
   Amount (+/-)
   Reason
   Target period (defaults to current/next)
- List pending adjustments
- Approve/Reject buttons

Rules:
- Staff can create + approve up to £200 absolute value per adjustment
- Admin unlimited
- All actions require reason and are audited.

C) APPLY ADJUSTMENTS IN PAYOUT RUN
Update payout run generation (Prompt 29):
- When generating a run for period X:
   Include all adjustments where:
     adjustment_status='approved'
     applied_in_period_id IS NULL
     AND (period_id == X OR period_id is prior and flagged to carry forward)
- Apply by:
   Adding to candidate gross_amount.
   shifts_count unchanged.
   Record that adjustment applied_in_period_id = X and status='applied'.
- Also show adjustments on staff table line as “Includes adjustments: £X”.

D) DISPUTE LINKAGE (STRUCTURE ONLY)
Add field on cmn_candidate_pay_approvals:
- dispute_flag TINYINT(1) DEFAULT 0
- dispute_note VARCHAR(255) NULL

Staff can set dispute_flag=1 which forces approval_status='disputed' and will exclude from run.
This does NOT delete anything.

E) AUDIT EVENTS
Log:
- payroll_adjustment_created
- payroll_adjustment_approved
- payroll_adjustment_rejected
- payroll_adjustment_applied
- pay_approval_marked_disputed

F) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
- Approved adjustment shows in next payout run.
- Adjustment marked applied once included.
- Disputed approvals excluded.
- Staff limits enforced; admin overrides allowed.

PROMPT 33 — Candidate “Query Pay” button → Support (UI + hook)

You are working inside the existing CoverMeNow ONE custom WordPress plugin.

Implement Prompt 33: Add a “Query Pay” action from Candidate Finance that submits a payroll query into the existing Support/Ticket system.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Candidate-only.
- Must not create duplicate spam tickets (basic throttling).
- Must link the ticket to the candidate, payroll period, and (optional) specific booking day(s).
- Must be auditable.

REQUIREMENTS

A) CANDIDATE FINANCE UI
On the Candidate Finance page:
- Add a “Query Pay” button in the Weekly Pay Overview card.
- Clicking opens a modal (or inline expandable form) with fields:
  1) Pay period (default current period_id; allow choosing previous 4 periods)
  2) Issue type (dropdown):
     - Missing shift
     - Incorrect rate
     - Hold / bank issue
     - Dispute no-show / attendance
     - Other
  3) Optional booking day selector (list booking days in the chosen period; multi-select)
  4) Message (required, min 10 chars)
  5) Attachments (optional, if your support system already supports uploads; otherwise omit)

B) SERVER ENDPOINT
Create secure handler (wp_ajax_*) that:
- Validates nonce
- Confirms role is candidate and user owns the period/booking data referenced
- Rate limits: max 2 payroll queries per 24h per candidate (transient)

C) SUPPORT TICKET CREATION
Use the existing ticket system in the portal:
- Create a ticket with:
  category = 'Payroll'
  priority = 'Normal'
  subject = "Payroll query — <period label>"
  body includes:
    - issue type
    - period_id + dates
    - booking day(s) if provided
    - candidate’s message

Store structured metadata (in your structured support tables, NOT post meta):
- candidate_user_id
- period_id
- booking_ids/dates array (nullable)
- payout_run_id if exists

D) AUDIT EVENTS
Log:
module='support'
entity_type='ticket'
event_type='payroll_query_ticket_created'
metadata={candidate_user_id, period_id, issue_type, ticket_id}

E) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
- Candidate can submit a query successfully.
- Non-candidate cannot.
- Ticket is created in Payroll category.
- Rate limit works.
- Ticket metadata is stored and retrievable.
- Audit event logged.
OUTPUT
- Provide code changes only with file locations.
- No explanations.

PROMPT 34 — Auto-route Payroll tickets + notify staff

You are working inside the existing CoverMeNow ONE custom WordPress plugin.

Implement Prompt 34: Automatically route Payroll tickets to the correct staff queue and notify internal staff email(s).

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Must not email schools/candidates from this prompt.
- Must avoid leaking sensitive data (no bank info ever).
- Must be auditable.

REQUIREMENTS

A) ROUTING RULES
When a ticket category = 'Payroll' is created:
- Assign it to payroll queue (or payroll tag/flag in your support system)
- Set default assignee:
  - If payroll duty user exists, assign to them
  - Else assign to Admin user
(Use your existing support assignment model; do not invent WP roles for routing if the system has its own.)

B) NOTIFICATION EMAIL
Send internal email from:
school@covermenow.co.uk
To:
- payroll@covermenow.co.uk (if exists in config) else fallback admin email

Subject:
"New Payroll Ticket — <ticket_id> — <period_id>"

Body (no bank info):
- Ticket ID + link to staff view
- Candidate name + user_id
- Period label
- Issue type
- Message preview (first 200 chars)

C) STAFF UI BADGE
In Staff Payroll page:
- Show badge/count of “Open payroll tickets” (query support system)

D) AUDIT EVENTS
Log:
module='support'
event_type='payroll_ticket_routed'
metadata={ticket_id, assigned_to, notification_sent:true/false}

E) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
- Payroll tickets auto-route correctly.
- Email notification sends with safe content.
- Staff payroll page shows open ticket count.
- Audit event logged.

OUTPUT
- Provide code changes only with file locations.
- No explanations.

PROMPT 35 — Staff filters + quick actions for Payroll tickets

You are working inside the existing CoverMeNow ONE custom WordPress plugin.

- Staff/Admin only.

- Must be auditable.
- Must integrate with existing support UI/components.

REQUIREMENTS

Add a “Payroll Tickets” panel (or sub-tab) accessible from Staff Payroll page:
- List open Payroll tickets with:
  Ticket ID | Candidate | Period | Issue Type | Status | Created | Assignee

B) FILTERS
Filters:
- Status: Open / Pending / Resolved / Closed
- Issue type
- Period (current + previous 8)
- Assignee (me / unassigned / any)

C) QUICK ACTIONS
For each ticket row:
- “Open” (go to ticket detail)
- “Mark Pending”
- “Resolve”
- “Close”
- “Request info from candidate” (creates a reply in the ticket thread using existing support messaging)

D) LINKAGE TO PAYROLL DATA (READ-ONLY)
On ticket detail view (staff):
- Show read-only payroll context card:

  - Gross amount

  - Any applied adjustments

Log:
module='support'
event_type one of:
- payroll_ticket_status_changed
- payroll_ticket_assigned
metadata includes {ticket_id, from_status, to_status, actor_user_id}

- Staff can filter payroll tickets.
- Quick actions update ticket status and log audits.
- Ticket detail shows payroll context without sensitive data.
- Non-staff blocked.

OUTPUT
- Provide code changes only with file locations.
- No explanations.

[X] Prompt 0017 | 2026-02-16T04:55:09.959Z | verified
You are working inside the existing CoverMeNow ONE custom WordPress plugin.

Implement: Admin Training/Demo Mode UI that allows:
1) setting a “virtual now” timestamp (used by payroll/partner/rewards logic),
2) launching demo triggers (e.g., payroll auto-lock) safely,
3) opening a training page in a new tab for demos.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Admin-only access (strict capability check).
- Must NOT change server time.
- Must NOT affect real users unless Training Mode is enabled.
- Must be auditable.
- Must be easy to disable (global kill switch).
- Must not expose WP internals to portal users.

GOAL
Create a “Training Mode” system consisting of:
- A secure admin page for enabling/disabling training mode
- A virtual clock override used by time-dependent functions (payroll period resolve, Thu 17:00 lock, reminders)
- A dedicated “Training Console” page (opens in a new tab) for demos

────────────────────────────────
A) CONFIG + STORAGE
────────────────────────────────

Create a new structured table (not post meta):

cmn_training_mode_state

Columns:
- id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY
- is_enabled TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
- virtual_now DATETIME NULL   (when null, use real time)
- scope VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'admin_only'
   allowed: 'admin_only' | 'all_users'
- updated_by_user_id BIGINT NULL
- updated_at DATETIME NOT NULL

Rules:
- Default is_enabled=0.
- Default scope='admin_only'.
- Only Admin can set scope='all_users' (and show warning).
- In normal operation, training mode should affect ONLY admins (scope admin_only).

────────────────────────────────
B) VIRTUAL CLOCK WRAPPER (CRITICAL)
────────────────────────────────

Create a helper used everywhere time matters:

cmn_now(): DateTimeImmutable

Logic:
- Load training state
- If training_mode disabled => return real now in WP timezone
- If enabled:
   - If scope='admin_only' and current user is NOT admin => return real now
   - If virtual_now set => return virtual_now (WP timezone)
   - Else return real now

Also add:
cmn_today_ymd(): string (Y-m-d)
cmn_wp_timezone(): DateTimeZone

Then update ALL time-dependent functions to use cmn_now()/cmn_today_ymd():
- payroll period resolution (Prompt 27)
- Thu 17:00 auto-lock cron logic (Prompt 28)
- any reminder scheduling logic that depends on “today/now”
- any reward academic year rollovers (if they used date comparisons)

NOTE:
Do not refactor the entire system; only replace calls where logic depends on current time.
Keep changes minimal and safe.

────────────────────────────────
C) ADMIN “TRAINING MODE” PAGE
────────────────────────────────

Add WP Admin menu:
Finance → “Training Mode”

Admin UI controls:
- Toggle: Enable Training Mode (on/off)
- Virtual Now input:
   - Date + time picker (WP timezone)
   - Buttons:
     - “Set Virtual Now”
     - “Clear Virtual Now”
- Scope selector:
   - Admin-only (default)
   - All users (admin only, show danger warning)

Add “Open Training Console” button:
- Opens a portal-styled page in a new tab:
  /?cmn_training_console=1

Security:
- nonce checks for every action
- capability checks for page and AJAX updates

Audit:
Log each change:
module='training'
event_type one of:
- training_enabled
- training_disabled
- training_virtual_now_set
- training_virtual_now_cleared
- training_scope_changed
metadata includes previous/new values and actor_user_id

────────────────────────────────
D) TRAINING CONSOLE PAGE (PORTAL STYLED)
────────────────────────────────

Create a new portal route:
/?cmn_training_console=1

Access:
- Admin only (or staff if you want, but default admin-only)
- Show “Training Console” header
- Show current:
  - Training enabled status
  - Virtual now (or real now)
  - Scope

Include “Demo Actions” buttons (safe triggers):
1) “Simulate Thu 17:00 Auto-Lock (Run Now)”
   - Executes the same function used by the cron (Prompt 28) but using cmn_now()
2) “Generate Payout Run for Current Period”
   - Calls payout run generator (Prompt 29) for current period_id
3) “Send School Booking Confirmation Reminders (Run Now)”
   - Calls reminder dispatcher (Partner Programme reminders)
4) “Generate Candidate Remittance PDFs for Current Period (Run Now)”
   - For candidates with payout items, generate docs (Prompt 32)

Each button:
- requires nonce
- logs audit event:
  module='training'
  event_type='training_action_run'
  metadata={action_name, period_id if relevant}

IMPORTANT SAFETY:
- If training scope is admin_only:
   actions MUST operate on:
   - either a configured allowlist of demo school/candidate IDs
   OR
   - only data created in “training mode” (see E)
Choose one approach below.

────────────────────────────────
E) SAFE DATA SCOPING (choose approach 1)
────────────────────────────────

Approach 1 (recommended):
Add a flag column to key operational tables to mark “training records”
Example: cmn_candidate_pay_approvals, cmn_payroll_payout_runs, remittance docs:
- is_training TINYINT(1) DEFAULT 0

When training mode is enabled AND scope=admin_only:
- any run/lock/docs created from Training Console must set is_training=1
- Training console lists/uses only is_training=1 objects unless scope=all_users
- This prevents demo triggers from touching real payroll artifacts.

(If adding is_training to every table is too heavy, apply it at minimum to:
- cmn_payroll_period_locks
- cmn_payroll_payout_runs + items
- cmn_candidate_remittance_docs
- cmn_payroll_adjustments
)

────────────────────────────────
F) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (comments)
────────────────────────────────
- Admin can enable training mode and set virtual time.
- Non-admin cannot access training pages or actions.
- cmn_now() returns virtual time for admin when enabled.
- payroll period resolution uses cmn_now().
- auto-lock cron uses cmn_now().
- training console can run auto-lock + generate payout run without errors.
- actions do not touch real artifacts when scope=admin_only (use is_training gating).
- all changes/actions are audited.

OUTPUT
- Provide code changes only with file locations.
- No explanations.

In the screenshot, you can see the notification area partially visible but layered under the calendar/cards.

Make the portal notification/toast/banner ALWAYS appear above all portal content (cards, calendar tiles, modals, sticky headers), readable, and clickable, without breaking layout.

REQUIREMENTS
- Production safe.
- Only affect portal UI, not wp-admin.
- Fix must be robust even if parent containers have transforms/filters/overflow that create stacking contexts.
- Must work on desktop + mobile.
- Do NOT “just add random z-index to everything” — fix the notification element specifically.
- Provide code changes only + file locations.

TASKS (DO IN ORDER)

1) Identify the notification element
- Reproduce the banner: open /portal/?candidate=calendar and trigger an action that shows the notification (e.g., Save Changes).
- Inspect DOM and identify the notification container element (id/class) being used (e.g. any “toast”, “notice”, “banner”, “alert”, “message” wrapper).
- Find where it is created (PHP render vs JS inject).
- Note if it lives inside a container that has:
  - transform / filter / perspective
  - overflow hidden/auto
  - position + z-index that creates a stacking context

2) Create a dedicated top-level portal overlay host (robust approach)
- Add a single overlay host near the end of the portal shell markup, directly inside <body> of the portal page output:
  <div id="cmn-portal-overlay-root"></div>
- Ensure it is only rendered for portal pages (not site homepage, not wp-admin).

3) Render/move notifications into the overlay host
- If notifications are injected by JS:
  - Update the JS so notifications are appended to #cmn-portal-overlay-root (not inside the page content).
- If notifications are server-rendered:
  - Render them inside #cmn-portal-overlay-root instead of inside inner content.
- If it’s easier, you may keep existing markup but “teleport” the element on display into the overlay root.

4) Apply correct overlay CSS (frontend.css)
Add CSS that guarantees it sits on top:

#cmn-portal-overlay-root {
  position: fixed;
  top: 0;
  left: 0;
  width: 100%;
  height: 0;           /* host only; notifications define their own height */
  z-index: 2147483000; /* very high but still safe */
  pointer-events: none; /* host ignores clicks except notification */
}

#cmn-portal-overlay-root .cmn-toast,
#cmn-portal-overlay-root .cmn-notice,
#cmn-portal-overlay-root .cmn-banner,
#cmn-portal-overlay-root .cmn-notification,
#cmn-portal-overlay-root [data-cmn-toast],
#cmn-portal-overlay-root [data-cmn-notice] {
  position: relative;
  pointer-events: auto;
  margin: 16px auto 0 auto;
  max-width: 920px;
}

- Use the REAL class/id you found in step 1 (do not rely on generic names).
- Ensure readable contrast (white text, etc.) but do not redesign, only fix layering.

5) Prevent clipping
- If the current notification lives inside an element with overflow hidden/auto and is getting clipped, remove that dependency by moving it into the overlay root (this should solve it).
- Do not globally remove overflow constraints across the app.

6) Acceptance checks
- Trigger notification on candidate calendar: notification must be fully visible and on top.
- Trigger notification on another portal page: still visible on top.
- Check it does not sit behind any modal.
- On mobile width, it should fit (use max-width + margins).

PRIORITY ALLOCATION SYSTEM (Geo-Restricted).

    'active'

Implement a new School Partner Programme (SPP) feature:
WEIGHTED ROUTING for candidate notifications / booking priority ordering.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Must NOT force bookings or auto-assign candidates.
- This is ordering/prioritisation only.
- Must be auditable.
- Must respect role permissions.
- Must not break existing booking request flow or notifications.
- Must not leak other schools’ actions to each other.
- Must be performant during morning peak loads.

GOAL
If multiple schools trigger booking actions / notifications to the same candidate around the same time, the candidate should see those schools ordered by SPP routing weight (highest first).

────────────────────────────
A) DEFINE ROUTING WEIGHTS (BY SPP TIER)
────────────────────────────

SPP routing levels and weights:

- Bronze: "Standard"           weight = 100
- Silver: "Priority"           weight = 200
- Gold:  "Top Priority"        weight = 300
- Elite: "Premier Priority"    weight = 400

Create function:
cmn_get_school_routing_weight($school_user_id)

Source:
- Use existing SPP tier state table (cmn_school_partner_state or equivalent).
- Default for non-partner/standard = 0 (or 50), but only apply weights for SPP tiers.

────────────────────────────
B) DETECT “COMPETING” REQUESTS TO SAME CANDIDATE
────────────────────────────

We consider “competing” as:
- Multiple booking requests/alerts for the same candidate
- within a short window (e.g., 15 minutes rolling window)
- where the candidate has not yet accepted/declined

Implement logic in the candidate-facing booking/notification list query:
- Load all active notifications/booking requests for candidate
- For each item, attach:
  - school_user_id
  - routing_weight
  - created_at

Sort order:
1) routing_weight DESC
2) created_at DESC (newest first within same weight)

Do NOT change database records just to sort.
Do NOT block lower-tier schools from appearing.

────────────────────────────
C) UI CHANGES (CANDIDATE SIDE)
────────────────────────────

On candidate notifications / booking list:
- Show a small label/badge next to the school name indicating routing level:
  - Standard / Priority / Top Priority / Premier Priority
- Keep it subtle and professional (not gamified).
- Badge colour should match portal styling (dark theme with red accents).

Do NOT expose other schools or counts. Just label each item.

────────────────────────────
D) AUDIT LOGGING
────────────────────────────

Whenever candidate notifications list is rendered (or when order is computed), do NOT spam logs.
Instead, log only when:
- Two or more schools are competing for the same candidate in the window
AND
- The ordering causes a non-default result (i.e., at least one item is moved above another due to weight)

Log one event per candidate per window:
module='school_partner'
entity_type='candidate'
entity_id=candidate_user_id
event_type='weighted_routing_applied'
metadata={
  window_minutes: 15,
  items_count: N,
  top_school_user_id: X,
  top_weight: W
}

────────────────────────────
E) SCHOOL SIDE VISIBILITY (OPTIONAL SIMPLE)
────────────────────────────

On the School Partner Programme dashboard (school view):
Add a small info card:
"Weighted Routing: Your tier gives you {routing_level} priority when schools compete for the same candidate."

No need to show weights, just the label.

────────────────────────────
F) ACCEPTANCE TESTS (AS COMMENTS)
────────────────────────────

- Candidate has two booking notifications from two different schools within 15 minutes.
- If one school is higher tier, its notification appears above the lower tier one.
- If both are same tier, newest appears first.
- If no competition, ordering unchanged.
- Candidate can still accept any booking (no auto-accept).
- No leaking of other schools’ actions.
- Performance acceptable with 5,000+ candidates and heavy morning traffic.

- Provide code changes only with file locations.
- No explanations.

Implement a production-ready School → Candidate Feedback system that is triggered after a booking is completed.

CONSTRAINTS
- Production-ready.
- Fully auditable.
- Role-based permissions strictly enforced.
- Feedback only allowed after booking is marked completed by the school (or system auto-completion where applicable).
- Candidates can view ALL feedback (modules + written comments).
- Schools can only submit feedback for their own completed bookings.
- Staff/Admin can view all feedback.
- No loose post meta; use structured DB tables.
- Must not break existing booking completion, payroll, or rewards logic.
- Must handle multi-day bookings: feedback is per booking day OR per booking (choose per booking unless day-level is already modeled).
- Prevent duplicate submissions (one feedback submission per booking per candidate).
- Include “additional comments” free text field.

GOAL
After a booking is completed, prompt the school to rate the candidate across 7 modules (1–5 stars each) plus optional written feedback. Store feedback, compute an average score, and surface it in candidate profile + school candidate cards.

────────────────────────────
A) FEEDBACK MODULES (7 x 1–5)
────────────────────────────
Store ratings for these modules:
1. Punctuality
2. Classroom Management
3. Professionalism
4. Communication
5. Adaptability
6. Lesson Delivery / Engagement
7. Safeguarding Awareness

Plus:
- Additional Comments (TEXT, optional)

Compute:
- average_rating = mean of the 7 module scores (rounded to 1 decimal for display; store raw average with 2 decimals)

────────────────────────────
B) DATABASE TABLES
────────────────────────────

1) Create table: cmn_candidate_feedback

Columns:
- id BIGINT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY
- booking_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- school_user_id BIGINT NOT NULL
- candidate_user_id BIGINT NOT NULL

Module scores (TINYINT 1–5 NOT NULL):
- score_punctuality
- score_classroom_management
- score_professionalism
- score_communication
- score_adaptability
- score_lesson_delivery
- score_safeguarding

- average_rating DECIMAL(3,2) NOT NULL
- comments TEXT NULL

- created_at DATETIME NOT NULL
- updated_at DATETIME NOT NULL

Indexes / constraints:
- UNIQUE KEY (booking_id, candidate_user_id)  // one submission per booking per candidate
- INDEX (candidate_user_id)
- INDEX (school_user_id)

2) (Optional, if you want faster reads) Create/extend a stats table:
cmn_candidate_rating_stats (if not already present)

Columns:
- candidate_user_id BIGINT PRIMARY KEY
- avg_rating DECIMAL(3,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
- feedback_count INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
- updated_at DATETIME NOT NULL

If not creating stats table, compute via query with caching, but must be performant.

────────────────────────────

Trigger:
When a booking is marked completed (by school or auto-completion policy),
send an email to the school and show an in-portal prompt:
"Rate your booking – help maintain quality and unlock partner benefits."

School can submit feedback via:
- Portal UI (preferred)
- Link from email to the feedback screen

UI requirements:
- Show booking details (date, role, candidate name)
- 7 star sliders (1–5)
- Additional comments textarea
- Submit button
- If already submitted: show “Feedback submitted” read-only view

Enforce:
- Only the school attached to the booking can submit.
- Only after booking completed.
- Block duplicates by unique key.

Audit log on submit:
module='feedback'
entity_type='booking'
entity_id=booking_id
event_type='candidate_feedback_submitted'
metadata={candidate_user_id, school_user_id, average_rating}

────────────────────────────
D) CANDIDATE VISIBILITY (CANDIDATE SIDE)
────────────────────────────

Candidates can view ALL feedback items.

Add a new tab/section in candidate portal:
"Feedback & Ratings"

Display:
- Overall average rating (large, e.g. 4.6/5)
- Total feedback count
- Module breakdown averages (optional but recommended)
- List of feedback entries:
   - school name (or masked if required later; for now show school name)
   - booking date
   - module scores
   - average for that entry
   - written comments (if provided)

────────────────────────────
E) SCHOOL CANDIDATE CARDS + ORDERING SUPPORT
────────────────────────────

- Show feedback count (e.g. “(12 reviews)”)

This system will be used for ranking.

F) STATS UPDATE (PERFORMANCE)
────────────────────────────

On each feedback submit:

- Store in cmn_candidate_rating_stats (if created)
OR
- Update cached/transient and ensure list queries remain fast.

Must be efficient for 5,000+ candidates.

G) EMAIL NOTIFICATION (SCHOOL)
────────────────────────────

After completion, send an email from school@covermenow.co.uk:
Subject: “Please confirm and rate your completed booking”
Body includes:
- Booking summary
- Link to feedback page
- Note: “Each completed feedback helps maintain quality and supports your School Partner Programme progress.”

Ensure email send uses existing email centre template system and logs.

- School cannot submit feedback for other schools’ bookings.
- Duplicate submissions blocked.
- Candidate can view all submitted feedback.

- Not on every shift beyond threshold

- Only apply new structure to future milestone triggers.

- Multiplier logic
- Rolling window logic
- Elite recurring logic

[X] Prompt 0163 | 2026-02-20T11:31:26.814Z | verified
CODEX TASK: Allow rejected schools to reapply with the same email (do not block on “email already registered”)

Context:
- We have a WordPress plugin (covermenow-one / covermenowone-one.php) handling school registration/application.
- Currently, if a school is rejected and tries to apply again with the same email, WP blocks it (email already exists).
- We WANT rejected schools to be able to reapply cleanly, without needing a new email.
- We do NOT want to create duplicate WP users for the same email.

Goal:
Implement a “reapply” flow:
- If email exists AND that school is rejected -> allow reapply (reset status to pending / requested, create a fresh access request entry, send notifications).
- If email exists AND school is active/pending -> show a clear message and do not create a new application.

Definitions (use existing structures where possible):
- A school WP user (or school entity) has a status stored in usermeta OR postmeta (whichever the plugin uses), e.g.:
  - cmn_school_status = "pending" | "approved" | "rejected" | "more_info"
  OR
  - access_request_status = similar values
- A rejection currently happens via an admin action “Reject” (or equivalent).

Implementation Requirements:
1) On School Application Submit:
   - Normalize/sanitize the email.
   - Try to locate an existing user by email (get_user_by('email', $email)).
   - If user does NOT exist:
     - Proceed with normal registration + application creation (no change).
   - If user EXISTS:
     - Determine if this existing user corresponds to a school entity in our system.
     - Read their current status (from the plugin’s canonical meta key(s)).

2) Reapply Rule:
   - If status == "rejected":
     - DO NOT attempt to create a new WP user.
     - Treat this as a REAPPLICATION:
       a) Set status back to "pending" (or "requested") and clear any rejection reason fields.
       b) Create a new access-request record/timeline entry (whatever the plugin uses) so we have a clean audit trail.
          - Include: timestamp, action="reapply", previous_status="rejected".
       c) Re-send the “new school application” notifications to admin/account manager.
       d) Show success UI: “Thanks — your application has been resubmitted for review.”
     - Make sure the school can proceed through the same onboarding steps as a new applicant.

3) If status is NOT rejected:
   - If status == "approved":
       Show message: “This email is already linked to an approved school account. Please log in.”
   - If status == "pending" or "more_info":
       Show message: “You already have an application in progress. Please check your email / portal messages.”
   - Do not create a duplicate application unless we explicitly want that (we do not).

4) Rejection Action (Admin side) – keep it as a soft state:
   - Confirm that “Reject” does NOT delete the WP user.
   - Store:
     - status="rejected"
     - rejection_reason (optional)
     - rejected_at timestamp
   - IMPORTANT: Do not lock the email out of future apply attempts.

5) Edge Cases:
   - If an email exists but is not a school user (e.g. admin/candidate):
     - Do NOT convert it into a school automatically.
     - Show a safe message: “This email is already in use. Please use a different email or contact support.”
     - Log it.

6) Logging:
   - Add log lines into System Health → School View Logs (or equivalent logging panel):
     - "school_reapply_detected" with {email, user_id, previous_status}
     - "school_reapply_submitted" with {email, user_id, new_status}
     - "school_apply_blocked_existing" with {email, user_id, status}

Acceptance Criteria:
- A rejected school can reapply using the same email without errors.
- No duplicate WP users are created.
- The application status resets to pending/requested.
- Admin notifications fire again.
- Attempts by approved/pending schools are blocked with helpful messaging.
- All actions are logged.

Deliverable:
- Implement in the plugin wherever the school application POST handler lives.
- Keep changes minimal and consistent with existing meta keys and request/timeline structures in the codebase.

[X] Prompt 0170 | 2026-02-20T12:57:37.860Z | verified
candidate single role + hidden rates

Context
CoverMeNow ONE WordPress plugin. We have “school” entities stored as WP posts (and/or custom tables) with a human-readable CMN school id like CMN0002. Two different schools currently share CMN0002. This causes profile view + status update actions to hang / crash and logs show memory exhaustion in class-wp-query.php line 5072 at stage resolve_by_pid.

GOALS (must all be done)

Enforce unique CMN School IDs forever (no duplicates possible going forward).

Add a safe backfill/migration to fix any duplicates already created.

Fix the resolver logic so no profile load or status update can trigger a huge WP_Query that exhausts memory.

If a school is rejected, they must be able to re-apply with the same email without “email already registered” blocking them.

Candidates must select exactly one role. For existing candidates, wipe all their roles and require re-selection. Notify them.

Candidate day rate must NOT appear anywhere on candidate profile pages—only in Finance.

1) Unique CMN School ID generation (forward fix)
Requirements

CMN IDs format: CMN + 4 digits (e.g., CMN0001, CMN0002…).

Generation must be atomic and must check uniqueness.

Store in a single authoritative place (existing meta key / column you already use, e.g. cmn_school_id).

Implementation

Create option cmn_school_seq (integer).

Function cmn_generate_next_school_id():

increment seq (atomic, use update_option loop with re-read)

create candidate id CMN%04d

check if any school already has that id (fast query returning IDs only, limit 1)

if exists, loop again

return unique id

Query all school posts with meta cmn_school_id.

For each other duplicate post:

record a mapping {old_id, new_id, post_id}.

Requirements

fields => 'ids'

no_found_rows => true

posts_per_page => 1

update_post_meta_cache => false

update_post_term_cache => false

If PID is missing/0:

Resolve by CMN ID using the safe query above.

If more than one match exists (should not after migration, but still):

pick the most recent OR oldest (choose one) deterministically

log resolve_ambiguous_school_id with count and chosen post_id

If resolver fails:

do not white-screen; return a controlled error screen with reference code already in your logging system.

Additional hard guard

If school_identifier looks like a numeric post_id (e.g., “1263”), treat it as a post_id and resolve directly with get_post() (no query).

[X] Prompt 0268 | 2026-02-21T18:42:49.182Z | verified
CODEX PROMPT — CoverMeNow ONE Admin UI: New Left Navigation + IA (with confirmation report)

GOAL
Redesign the ADMIN portal left-side navigation and route structure to match the new information architecture. This is an IA + UI navigation refactor (not a full page redesign), but it must be stable, consistent, and not break existing workflows.

CONTEXT
We currently have a left navigation with many scattered items. We want grouped sections with clear labels:
- Schools (with subviews: all schools, leads, applications, active clients, etc.)
- Candidates
- Bookings
- Commercial (finance)
- Support Hub (new top-level)
- Intelligence (analytics hub)
- Automation
- System
- Configuration (admin settings)

CRITICAL BUSINESS RULES
1) “Clients” is NOT a separate entity type. A Client is a School with lifecycle/status = active client.
   - We still need an admin action to “Convert to Client” (or “Approve & Convert to Client”) from an application/leads flow.
   - That action must update the same underlying school record + status/lifecycle fields and trigger the same emails/notifications already implemented for status changes.
2) Contacts should NOT be a global standalone section unless essential.
   - Contacts should live inside each School profile (tabbed/sectioned), because cross-school contacts are rare.
   - If there is an existing Contacts page, keep it accessible only if it already has dependencies, but hide it from the primary nav (put behind Configuration or inside School Profile as “Contacts” tab).
3) Compliance Reviews: manual approval for now.
   - Candidate documentation is approved during video call; keep “Compliance Reviews” in Candidates as an admin tool, but it should be clearly marked “Manual review”.
4) No page should “lose navigation” when opening logs/errors. Left nav must remain visible across all admin views.

NAV STRUCTURE (MUST IMPLEMENT)
Left nav should be grouped with section headers (not just a flat list). Use icons if already supported.

A) Dashboard
- Dashboard (home)

B) Schools
- All Schools
- Leads
- Applications (Pending / More Info Needed)
- Active Clients
- Rejected / Archived (optional, can be a filtered view)

Inside School Profile (single school page), use tabs/sections:
- Overview
- Notes
- Tasks
- Contacts
- Activity / Timeline
- Documents (optional)
- Commercial (what we charge; internal only)
- Status / Lifecycle actions (approve, more info, reject, convert to client)

C) Candidates

GOAL
Upgrade the Schools “All Schools” screen to support:
1) A universal search box that searches across multiple school/contact/account-manager fields.
2) Date-based filtering for “Last Activity / Contacted” with operators: Never, Before, After, Between.
Keep the existing UI style and do NOT break existing routes, bulk actions, or table layout.

CONSTRAINTS
- No data model changes.
- Manual approval flow unchanged.
- Keep existing columns and “View” button.
- Keep existing status/pipeline pills.
- Filters must work for All Schools, Leads, Active Clients, Rejected/Archived views too (same search/filter component reused).
- Do not remove the current Search / Filters / Clear buttons; upgrade what they do.

IMPLEMENTATION DETAILS

A) Universal Search (single text box)
- Rename placeholder from “Search schools...” to “Search schools, contacts, location, email, phone, account manager...”
- When user types a query Q and clicks Search (or presses Enter), filter results where Q matches ANY of:
  - School name (post_title)
  - Location fields (town/city meta)
  - Primary contact name meta
  - Primary contact phone meta
  - Primary contact email meta
  - School email meta
  - Account manager display_name (resolved from assigned user id) OR stored account manager name fields
- Matching should be case-insensitive and partial (contains).
- If Q is empty, do not apply this filter.

B) Date Filter: “Contacted / Last Activity”
In the Filters panel add a section called “Last Activity” with:
- Operator dropdown: [Any, Never contacted, Before, After, Between]
- Date input(s):
  - Before: show one date input
  - After: show one date input
  - Between: show two date inputs (start/end)
  - Never contacted: no date inputs
- Apply this filter using the SAME “Last Activity” value already shown in the table (the computed timestamp used for the Last Activity column).
  - Never contacted means: no recorded activity timestamps and no qualifying booking timestamps (i.e., treat as null/empty).
  - Before/After/Between compares the computed timestamp.

C) UX
- Filters button toggles an expanded filters panel (same style).
- Clear button clears Q and all filter selections, then reloads unfiltered list.
- Show a small inline “Active filters” text (optional) like: “Filtered: Account Manager = Unassigned, Last Activity = Before 2026-02-01”
- Preserve mobile behavior (table scroll ok).

ROUTES / QUERY PARAMS
Use query params so filters are shareable:
- q= for universal search
- cmn_last_activity_op=any|never|before|after|between
- cmn_last_activity_start=YYYY-MM-DD
- cmn_last_activity_end=YYYY-MM-DD
Also preserve existing cmn_status and other existing params.

FILES
Update the minimum necessary files, likely:
- wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/covermenowone-one.php (render + query building)
- wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/frontend.css (only if needed for filter layout)

CODE REQUIREMENTS
- Implement filtering safely using WP_Query/meta_query where possible.
- If account manager display_name searching can’t be done directly in meta_query, implement a safe fallback:
  - Resolve matching user IDs for Q via WP_User_Query on display_name, then match schools by assigned manager user id meta.
- Prevent SQL injection; sanitize all input.
- Add helper functions if needed: cmn_get_school_last_activity_ts(), cmn_school_matches_universal_search()

NOW RETURN A “CODEX CONFIRMATION REPORT” WITH:

We have a bug in CoverMeNow ONE:

Issue:
When an admin edits a school profile and adds/updates the “Cover Manager Email” for a school (test account), the save request sometimes returns “504 Gateway Timeout”. This likely means the request is blocking on something slow (DB lock, remote call, email workflow, validation, or large synchronous side-effect).

Task:
1) Reproduce and isolate
- Identify the exact endpoint hit when saving school profile fields (especially cover_manager_email).
- Capture request payload and response.
- Check server logs (nginx/apache + php-fpm) and application logs for the timed-out request.
- Confirm if this only happens when editing cover_manager_email or any field save.
- Confirm whether the timeout happens before DB commit or after commit (i.e., is the data saved despite the 504?).

2) Instrumentation and diagnostics
- Add timing logs around:
  a) input validation
  b) DB query/transaction
  c) any workflow triggers (notifications, geocode/location verify, email sends, audit log writes)
  d) any external API calls (geocoding, email provider, etc.)
- Log: school_id, staff_user_id, start/end timestamps, slow query info.
- Turn on slow query logging if available.

3) Fix the root cause (do NOT “increase timeout” as the primary fix)
Common likely causes and required changes:
A) Slow DB query / missing index
- Inspect queries that run when updating school contact fields.
- Add indexes for any WHERE clauses used in update/lookup (e.g., schools.id, schools.school_id, contacts.school_id, cover_manager_email).
- Ensure updates are single-row updates with proper primary key usage.
- Avoid SELECT * joins on update paths.

B) Synchronous side-effects causing blocking
- If updating cover_manager_email triggers:
  - sending email,
  - running “location verification”,
  - recalculating pipeline stats,
  - generating tasks,
  - writing big audit logs,
  move those side effects to async/background queue (or at least defer until after responding).
- The HTTP request must respond quickly (<500ms target, <2s max).

C) Deadlocks/transactions
- Ensure the update uses a short transaction.
- Avoid holding DB locks while doing non-DB work.
- Use “commit then dispatch side effects”.

D) Validation calling external services
- If any validation checks MX records, domain checks, geocode checks, etc. during save, remove from synchronous path.
- Validate format only on save; do deeper checks asynchronously.

4) Improve the UX resilience
- Frontend should show a proper error state when save fails.
- If backend did actually save but timed out while doing side effects, the UI should refresh the latest data after error and show “Saved, but background processing delayed” (only if we detect commit happened).
- Implement idempotency: repeated save attempts should not duplicate side effects (emails, tasks, logs).

5) Add tests + guardrails
- Add a regression test for updating cover_manager_email:
  - It should update the DB quickly and return 200.
  - Side effects (if any) should be queued, not blocking.
- Add monitoring: report any request >2s for this endpoint, include payload keys.

Deliverables:
- Identify the specific slow operation causing 504 (with evidence from logs/timings).
- Patch with the correct fix (indexing, removing blocking side effects, async queue).
- Confirm the save endpoint returns within 2 seconds reliably even under load.
- Confirm no duplicate notifications/actions occur after repeated retries.

Notes:
- Cover manager name/email are optional. If provided, school email should default to the cover manager email (populate if empty) but this should not trigger any heavy process during the save request.

Problem
- In Bulk Upload Schools, after uploading a CSV/XLSX and going to “Map spreadsheet columns to fields”, the dropdowns do not map/pull through correctly and the preview/import does not reflect the selected mappings.
- The UI shows a list preview step, then a mapping step with many fields (School Name*, School ID, Email, Phone, Location, Town/City, Address lines, County, Postcode, Website, Status, Pipeline Stage, Notes, Primary Contact Name, Cover Manager Name, Cover Manager Email).
- We need mapping to be robust and actually used to build the import payload.

Goal
1) Correctly parse CSV and XLSX.
2) Detect headers + sample values.
3) Provide AUTO-MAP suggestions (best match) for each field.
4) When user changes mapping dropdowns, the selected mapping MUST immediately affect the preview rows and the final import.
5) “Continue” must import using the selected mapping with correct validation and clear errors.

Implementation requirements
A) Parsing
- CSV: handle UTF-8 BOM, commas, semicolons, quoted fields, newlines, Windows line endings.
- XLSX: read first sheet by default; allow future extension for sheet selection (but not required now).
- Trim header whitespace; normalize case; collapse multiple spaces; remove surrounding quotes.
- Create a canonical “column key” for each header:
  normalizeHeader(h) = lowercased, trimmed, spaces/underscores/hyphens collapsed to single underscore, remove punctuation.
  Example: “Contact Number” -> “contact_number”; “School Name” -> “school_name”.

B) Auto-mapping
- Build a dictionary of synonyms for each target field. Examples:
  School Name: [school, school_name, establishment, establishment_name, name]
  School ID: [school_id, id, establishment_id, urn, dfes, ukprn]
  Email: [email, school_email, contact_email]
  Phone: [phone, telephone, tel, contact_number, phone_number]
  Location: [location, area, locality]
  Town/City: [town, city, town_city]
  Address Line 1: [address1, address_line_1, address_line1, line1]
  Address Line 2: [address2, address_line_2, address_line2, line2]
  County: [county, region]
  Postcode: [postcode, post_code, zip]
  Website: [website, url, web]
  Status: [status, cmn_status]
  Pipeline Stage: [pipeline, pipeline_stage, stage]
  Notes: [notes, note, comments]
  Primary Contact Name: [primary_contact, primary_contact_name, contact_name]
  Cover Manager Name: [cover_manager, cover_manager_name, cm_name]
  Cover Manager Email: [cover_manager_email, cm_email]
- Use a scoring function:
  exact match on normalized key = 100
  contains match = 70
  fuzzy match (levenshtein/jaro) = 40–65
  If multiple candidates, pick best score; if tie, pick leftmost column.
- Auto-select these in dropdowns on load.
- Always require School Name to be mapped; if not found, show a blocking validation message.

C) State + UI correctness
- Store mapping state in a single source of truth (e.g., React state or equivalent).
- When a dropdown changes:
  - update mapping state
  - regenerate preview rows (first N rows) using the mapping
  - ensure “Continue” uses the current mapping state (not stale, not default)
- Ensure each column can only be mapped to one field at a time (optional but strongly preferred):
  - if user selects a column already used elsewhere, either:
    - auto-unassign from previous field, or
    - prevent selection + show tooltip (“Already used for Email”).
- Add a “Reset to auto-map” button.

D) Preview step
- Show 10–20 preview rows using the mapped fields (not raw spreadsheet columns).
- If a mapped column is empty for a row, show “—”.
- Highlight missing required values (School Name) in preview.

E) Import payload + validation
- On “Continue”, build row objects using mapping:
  rowOut[fieldKey] = rowRaw[mappedColumnKey]
- Validation rules:
  - School Name: required, min length 2
  - Email: if present must look like email
  - Postcode: if present trim and uppercase
  - Phone: if present trim
- Show summary before final import:
  total rows, valid rows, invalid rows, with downloadable error CSV (optional if easy).
- Import should ignore blank rows.

F) Logging / debugging
- Add structured debug logs to the existing admin logs:
  - parsedHeaders (normalized + original)
  - autoMapResult
  - mappingChanges
  - previewBuild
  - importSummary + first 3 errors
- Make sure logs do not leak sensitive data beyond what admin already sees.

1) Column mapping reliably works (correct fields populated, deterministic mapping, correct import payload).
2) The mapping modal is fully scrollable on desktop + mobile (currently you cannot scroll down to see the preview and/or continue).
3) The mapped preview table renders correctly (no broken wrapping; long values don’t destroy layout).
4) The UI remains consistent with the rest of app.portal (same card + dark theme), and is responsive/mobile compatible.

CONTEXT / CURRENT BUGS:
- After uploading a CSV/XLSX and going to the mapping step, the system either:
  - fails to pull the headers correctly, OR
  - applies the wrong column to the wrong field, OR
  - loses the mapping between steps, OR
  - shows the preview but import doesn’t reflect the mapping.
- The mapping modal cannot be scrolled to the bottom (especially on smaller screens). The page behind scrolls or scroll is trapped incorrectly.
- Some preview cells wrap unpredictably (e.g., email breaks, location breaks) and columns become unreadable.

REQUIREMENTS:
A) Mapping correctness
- Parse CSV/XLSX headers robustly (trim whitespace, normalise case, handle duplicate headers, handle blank headers).
- Store mapping as a stable object: { fieldKey: columnIndex } (not just header name) and persist it through steps.
- Ensure required fields (School Name) must be mapped, with clear error UI if missing.
- When user changes a mapping dropdown, immediately recompute preview from raw rows using the mapping object (no stale cache).
- Ensure import payload uses the mapping object to map each row.
- Add validation: email format check (if mapped), phone normalisation (optional), ignore empty rows.
- If a column appears like an email/phone/postcode, provide “smart suggestions” in the dropdown default selection, BUT user choice always wins.

B) Modal scroll / UX
- Make the Bulk Upload a true modal with:
  - fixed header (title + Close)
  - scrollable body (mapping fields + preview)
  - fixed footer (Back + Continue/Import)
- Prevent background scroll when modal is open (body { overflow: hidden }).
- Allow internal modal scroll via max-height: calc(100vh - header - footer) and overflow-y: auto.
- On mobile: modal should be full-screen (or near full-screen) and always scrollable.

C) Preview table usability
- Preview area shows first 10 rows of mapped output.
- Make preview horizontally scrollable if needed (overflow-x: auto).
- Keep columns readable:
  - use table-layout: fixed; 
  - set reasonable min-widths;
  - ellipsis for long strings;
  - allow user to click a cell to expand or show full value via tooltip.
- Ensure the preview container does not push the modal beyond viewport.

D) Code quality
- Locate the bulk upload schools components (UI + parser + mapper + importer).
- Add/extend unit-ish tests (or lightweight test functions) for:
  - header normalisation
  - mapping persistence
  - mapping -> preview transform
  - mapping -> import transform
- Add minimal logging to help debug (only in admin, and behind a debug flag if available).

DELIVERABLES:
1) Identify the exact files/components responsible for:
   - file upload parsing
   - mapping screen rendering
   - preview generation
   - import request construction
2) Implement fixes for mapping + preview correctness.
3) Implement modal scroll/focus/overflow fixes (desktop + mobile).
4) Provide a short “How to test” checklist at the end.

DO NOT:
- Do not change unrelated portal pages.
- Do not introduce new heavy dependencies unless already present.
- Do not break the current theme; keep styles consistent.

START BY:

But on the FINAL step (the “Import Schools” confirmation list), the table shows ONLY School Name and all other mapped fields display “—”. This indicates the mapping is not being applied/propagated into the final preview payload or the final preview renderer is reading the wrong keys.

Also: the modal sometimes cannot scroll to the bottom (especially on smaller screens), and overall Admin/Staff dashboard needs to be mobile compatible and “super smooth”.

Your job
1) Fix the Bulk Upload Schools mapping so mapped columns carry through ALL steps, including the final confirmation table and the actual import payload.

Hard requirements (acceptance criteria)

Admin/Staff dashboard mobile UX

  - Collapses into a hamburger/drawer on < 1024px
  - Drawer overlays content, closes on selection, preserves current page
- Tables:

- Forms:

  - max-width responsive, max-height with internal scroll
  - focus trap + ESC closes

- No visual regressions to existing dark/glass theme.

[X] Prompt 0408 | 2026-02-24T13:46:38.899Z | verified
Micro-polish pass (sharpness + “premium” finish)
1) Make text crisp everywhere

Body text: 14–15px, line-height 1.45–1.6

Headings: slightly tighter line-height (1.15–1.25)

Avoid very thin weights on dark backgrounds (use 500/600 for labels, 600/700 for titles)

Add subtle global text rendering:

html { -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased; -moz-osx-font-smoothing: grayscale; }
2) Fix the “soft blur” look from the glass overlay

A lot of your cards look like they’re using a heavy blur + low contrast. Use a lighter blur and stronger edges:

Backdrop blur: 6–10px (not 20+)

Card fill: slightly darker but more opaque

Add a 1px border that’s visible, not “vibes only”

Suggested card style:

.card {
  background: rgba(10, 10, 12, 0.62);
  backdrop-filter: blur(8px);
  -webkit-backdrop-filter: blur(8px);
  border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.08);
  box-shadow: 0 10px 30px rgba(0,0,0,0.45);
  border-radius: 14px;
}
3) Consistent spacing rhythm (this is the “premium” switch)

Pick one system and stick to it:

Page padding: 24px

Card padding: 20px

Gaps between cards: 16px

Section gaps: 24–32px

Row height for list items: 44–48px

4) Buttons: reduce “big red slabs” + improve hierarchy

Right now the red buttons often feel oversized/heavy.

Primary: red, but shorter height (40–44px) and cleaner radius

Secondary: neutral outline / soft fill

Destructive: red outline + confirm step (esp. “Delete account”)

Suggested:

.btn { height: 42px; border-radius: 10px; font-weight: 600; }
.btn-primary { background:#d02a2a; color:#fff; }
.btn-secondary { background: rgba(255,255,255,0.06); border:1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.10); }
.btn-danger { background: transparent; border:1px solid rgba(208,42,42,0.55); color:#ffb3b3; }
5) Inputs + dropdowns: give them a real “edge”

A lot of fields look slightly washed out.

Use slightly higher contrast borders

Add clear focus ring (red-tinted but subtle)

.input, select, textarea{
  background: rgba(255,255,255,0.04);
  border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.10);
  border-radius: 10px;
  height: 42px;
}
.input:focus, select:focus, textarea:focus{
  outline: none;
  border-color: rgba(208,42,42,0.55);
  box-shadow: 0 0 0 3px rgba(208,42,42,0.18);
}
6) Labels + metadata alignment (big win on Profile / Finance / Support)

Make label text smaller + dimmer (12px–13px)

Values slightly brighter and aligned consistently

Use “key/value rows” with equal spacing so it looks deliberate

7) Chips / badges (VERIFIED, ON HOLD, NOT AVAILABLE YET)

Make them feel like a system:

Same height (24–26px)

Same radius (999px)

Same font size (12px)

Same padding (8–10px horizontal)

Avoid neon colors; keep them muted but readable

.badge { height: 24px; padding:0 10px; border-radius:999px; font-size:12px; font-weight:700; }
.badge-ok { background: rgba(46,160,90,0.18); border:1px solid rgba(46,160,90,0.35); color:#bff5cf; }

[X] Prompt 0412 | 2026-02-24T14:09:08.187Z | verified
2) Why schools are showing in Applications after adding a number (and how to fix it)

This is almost certainly a status transition bug caused by a “completion rule” like:

“If school has X required fields (e.g., phone), then treat as an application/request.”

So when you added a phone number to a record that was in Needs Attention, your system likely:

marked it as “complete enough”, and/or

fired a hook that creates a school_request/application row, or

flipped a status flag that your Applications query uses.

What should happen (clean model)

You want two separate concepts:

A) School lifecycle status

Lead → Active Client → Archived

B) Onboarding/Application workflow

Only created when the school submits an application (or you explicitly convert a lead into an onboarding request)

Those should not auto-trigger just because a field is filled.

The likely culprit (exact pattern I’ve seen)

Import puts schools into needs_attention = 1

Later you update phone → code says:

“if needs_attention is now resolved → status = pending”

OR “if phone exists AND (some other condition) → create request”

Applications list query is WHERE status = 'pending' or WHERE request_id IS NOT NULL

Fix (the rule)

Never create or move to “Application/Pending” based on field completeness alone.

Instead:

Only create an application when:

source = “public signup form” OR

user clicks “Create Application / Start Onboarding” in admin

Concrete changes to implement

1) Lock down creation

Remove/disable any “on update” logic like:

if phone != '' then create_request()

if required_fields_complete then status='pending'

Replace with explicit action:

Button: “Start onboarding”

That action creates a request/application record and sets request status.

2) Fix the Applications query
Applications should list only real onboarding requests, not “records that look complete”.

Use:

WHERE requests.created_from IN ('signup_form','manual_start')

or WHERE requests.id IS NOT NULL

Avoid:

WHERE schools.status='pending' if “pending” can be reached by other means.

3) Data cleanup
For the wrongly moved schools:

Delete the accidental request rows (or mark them void)

Move schools back to:

status = 'lead'

needs_attention = 0/1 depending on whether you still need review

request_id = NULL if you have that linkage

4) Add a guardrail
Add a field like:

request_origin (import/manual/signup)
Then Applications page filters to only:

request_origin != 'import_autofill'

Quick win UI tweak for the Applications screen

On that right panel (“Review Application”), show:

“No application selected” (fine)

and a warning banner if the selected row has origin=import:

“Imported record — not a submitted application.”

That makes these mistakes instantly visible.

If you paste the bit of code (or SQL) that decides:

what counts as an “application”, and/or

the query behind the Applications page,
I’ll tell you exactly which condition is causing the misrouting and the cleanest patch.

› 2) Why schools are showing in Applications after adding a number (and how to fix it)

  This is almost certainly a status transition bug caused by a “completion rule” like:

  “If school has X required fields (e.g., phone), then treat as an application/request.”

  So when you added a phone number to a record that was in Needs Attention, your system likely:

  marked it as “complete enough”, and/or

  fired a hook that creates a school_request/application row, or

  flipped a status flag that your Applications query uses.

  What should happen (clean model)

  You want two separate concepts:

  A) School lifecycle status

  Lead → Active Client → Archived

  B) Onboarding/Application workflow

  Only created when the school submits an application (or you explicitly convert a lead into an onboarding request)

  Those should not auto-trigger just because a field is filled.

  The likely culprit (exact pattern I’ve seen)

  Import puts schools into needs_attention = 1

  Later you update phone → code says:

  “if needs_attention is now resolved → status = pending”

  OR “if phone exists AND (some other condition) → create request”

  Applications list query is WHERE status = 'pending' or WHERE request_id IS NOT NULL

  Fix (the rule)

  Never create or move to “Application/Pending” based on field completeness alone.

  Instead:

  Only create an application when:

  source = “public signup form” OR

  user clicks “Create Application / Start Onboarding” in admin

  Concrete changes to implement

  1) Lock down creation

  Remove/disable any “on update” logic like:

  if phone != '' then create_request()

  if required_fields_complete then status='pending'

  Replace with explicit action:

  Button: “Start onboarding”

  That action creates a request/application record and sets request status.

  2) Fix the Applications query
  Applications should list only real onboarding requests, not “records that look complete”.

  Use:

  WHERE requests.created_from IN ('signup_form','manual_start')

  or WHERE requests.id IS NOT NULL

  Avoid:

  WHERE schools.status='pending' if “pending” can be reached by other means.

  3) Data cleanup
  For the wrongly moved schools:

  Delete the accidental request rows (or mark them void)

  Move schools back to:

  status = 'lead'

  needs_attention = 0/1 depending on whether you still need review

  request_id = NULL if you have that linkage

  4) Add a guardrail
  Add a field like:

  request_origin (import/manual/signup)
  Then Applications page filters to only:

  request_origin != 'import_autofill'

  Quick win UI tweak for the Applications screen

  On that right panel (“Review Application”), show:

  “No application selected” (fine)

  and a warning banner if the selected row has origin=import:

  “Imported record — not a submitted application.”

  That makes these mistakes instantly visible.

  If you paste the bit of code (or SQL) that decides:

  what counts as an “application”, and/or

  the query behind the Applications page,
  I’ll tell you exactly which condition is causing the misrouting and the cleanest patch.


• I’ll implement this now by tracing the school-save transition logic and the Applications query, then patching it so field edits (like phone/email) cannot auto-create/move records into Applications unless
  onboarding is explicitly started. I’m starting with a code search for request creation + status flips on school updates.

• I found the core school profile save path and bulk import handlers. Next I’m inspecting import_bulk_school_row() and the school lifecycle/access-status helpers to identify exactly where records are being moved
  into pending/application scope on field edits/import.

• I’ve isolated the core bug: the School Profile form defaults Request Status to pending even when no application exists, so saving any field can mark a lead as an application. I’m patching that default +
  server-side guard + Applications query so only real request records appear.

• I’ve patched the root status-routing logic and syntax-checked successfully. Next I’m deploying this fix so your Applications list only contains real onboarding requests and phone/email edits won’t misroute
  leads anymore.

• I’ve finished the patch and confirmed PHP syntax is valid. I’m deploying this now so editing lead fields no longer auto-moves schools into Applications.

• I’m adding one more safeguard to clean existing misrouted rows: if a school has cmn_access_request_status=pending but has no request markers (registration_ref/requested_at), it will no longer be treated as a
  real application. Then I’ll redeploy.

› CODEX TASK: UI “Makeover” pass (Candidate portal + Rewards + Profile) + Admin bug fix (Schools wrongly showing in Applications)

  Context
  - We have two visual styles right now: Admin looks clean/black/sharp; Candidate portal looks more “maroon haze / soft”.
  - I want Candidate portal to match Admin’s visual language: darker/cleaner/black-first, sharper edges, clearer hierarchy, less “stacked”.
  - Also: schools imported in a data upload are incorrectly appearing under Admin → Schools → Applications (as PENDING “requests”). These are NOT real applications/requests and must stay as Leads.

  PART A — VISUAL: global “micro-polish for sharpness”
  Goal: make everything feel crisp, premium, high-contrast, and consistent.

  1) Global tokens (CSS variables)
  - Introduce/adjust design tokens so candidate pages match admin:
    - Base background: near-black (#07090d to #0b0f14 range)
    - Card surface: slightly lifted dark (#0e131b / #111826)
    - Borders: subtle but visible (rgba(255,255,255,0.06–0.10))
    - Text: primary #e9eef7, secondary rgba(233,238,247,0.72)
    - Accent red: keep existing CMN red but make it “clean” (no muddy gradients)
  - Reduce “hazy maroon” background glow/overlay. Keep only a very subtle brand tint in corners (low opacity).

  2) Card styling
  - Standardize all cards:
    - border-radius consistent (e.g. 14px)
    - tighter shadow (less blur; more definition)
    - remove excessive inner glow
    - consistent padding (e.g. 24px)
    - headings: stronger size/weight; body text slightly smaller
  - Make dividers more consistent (1px rgba white 0.06).

  3) Buttons (critical)
  - Primary CTA must always win visually:
    - stronger contrast (solid red or red gradient very subtle)
    - add a thin highlight edge for crispness (1px rgba white 0.10) OR subtle top sheen, NOT glow.
    - hover/focus states: clear and snappy
  - Secondary buttons: neutral dark, clear border, not competing.

  4) Typography / spacing
  - Increase line-height slightly for readability.
  - Reduce vertical “dead space” between stacked sections.
  - Align titles, actions and badges to a consistent grid.

  Deliverable for PART A

    - Candidate Profile

  PART B — Candidate Dashboard: “Availability Confirmation” hero section
  Problem: CTA felt weaker, needs to stand out and be centered.
  Fix:
  - Center the primary confirmation CTA within the hero card.
  - Make it the visual focal point:
    - wider (but not full bleed), larger font, stronger fill.
    - keep “Opens in…” and “Why confirm?” as secondary supporting elements near the CTA, but not stealing attention.
  - Reduce clutter: remove duplicate explanatory text (keep it once).
  - Ensure status pill (“Not confirmed yet”) is clear but secondary.
  - The whole hero should feel like: Title → short instruction → BIG CTA → secondary actions.

  PART C — Candidate Dashboard layout: less stacked, more “admin-like”

    - Upcoming Booking + Booking History side-by-side

  - Keep live chat bubble from overlapping key buttons (add padding-bottom or reposition).

  PART D — Candidate Profile page: “wider boxes, more info, less stacked”
  Problems:

  - Save/Cancel bar feels awkward and blocks view while scrolling.

  PART F — ADMIN BUG: Schools incorrectly in Applications after data upload / adding a number
  Issue summary
  - After data upload, some schools were correctly in “Needs Attention”.
  - After adding a phone number (or similar edit), they got pushed into Schools → Applications as if they are pending requests.
  - This is wrong: they should remain a Lead (or Needs Attention), NOT a pending application/request.

  What to do
  1) Identify the classification logic that decides whether a school record is:
  - Lead
  - Application/Request (Pending)
  - Active client
  - Rejected/Archived
  - Needs Attention

  2) Confirm the intended rule:
  - Only schools that explicitly submitted an application/request via the proper flow should appear in Applications.
  - Data-imported schools should default to Lead (or Needs Attention) until they truly apply.
  - Editing a field (like phone) must NEVER create/flip a “pending request” state.

  3) Fix
  - Prevent side-effects on update: updating contact fields must not change request/application state.
  - Add a guard in update handler:
    - if record was created via import OR has no “application/request” origin marker, do not set status to PENDING/request.
  - Ensure Applications query filters only records that have a real application/request flag (e.g. source='application', has_request=1, request_submitted_at not null, etc.)

  4) Migration / repair
  - Move any wrongly-classified imported schools out of Applications back to Lead (or Needs Attention) based on a reliable signal:
    - created_by_import = 1 OR source='import' OR request_submitted_at is null
  - Do not lose audit history.

  Deliverables for PART F
  - Identify exact cause (which update path flips status).
  - Patch logic + add a regression check.
  - Provide a quick note: “Applications now only shows true application records.”

  Acceptance criteria
  - Candidate pages visually match Admin: darker, cleaner, crisp borders, consistent spacing.
  - Availability CTA is centered and obviously the main action.
  - Profile page is wider, less stacked, better edit UX.
  - Rewards page matches the same makeover.
  - Imported schools never appear in Applications unless they truly applied; editing a phone number cannot create a request.

    5) Admin bug fix + migration + regression check
