─ Worked for 1m 29s ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Implemented. The rule is now enforced in code:

  - Imported/needs-attention schools will not enter Applications just because phone/email/etc is edited.
  - A school is only treated as an Application/Pending request if there is an explicit request signal:
      - cmn_registration_requested_at or cmn_registration_ref, or
      - explicit manual-start marker cmn_access_request_manual_started_at from staff action.
  - Otherwise request state is cleared and the record stays in Lead/Needs Attention workflow.

  ### What I changed

  - wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/covermenowone-one.php
      - Added strict guards:
          - is_import_origin_school()
          - clear_school_access_request_state()
          - assert_imported_school_not_application_without_submission()
      - Hardened get_school_access_request_status() so imported records without submission/manual markers cannot resolve to pending.
      - Updated staff update paths to only create pending application state when explicitly started, and to stamp cmn_access_request_manual_started_at.
      - Added repair pass for already misclassified imported schools:
          - repair_imported_school_application_states()
          - maybe_repair_imported_school_application_states()
      - Repair is invoked in school-requests queue render/count paths, so stale bad records are moved out of Applications automatically.

  ### Result

  - Adding a number (or other profile field) no longer promotes imported schools into Applications.
  - Misclassified imported records are corrected back out of request state and stay in Leads/Needs Attention unless explicitly submitted.

  If you want, I can now run a one-time forced repair trigger path so all existing misclassified records are corrected immediately in one pass.

  sharper, cleaner), with improved spacing, alignment, and hierarchy.

  GOALS (visual):
  - Make the base feel more BLACK and neutral (less maroon wash). Match the admin dashboard look.
  - Improve “micro-polish”: sharper edges, consistent spacing, better typography contrast, less blur/fog.
  - Reduce “stacked” layouts: use wider cards, better use of horizontal space, fewer tall columns, more information visible without scrolling.
  - Primary CTA buttons must stand out strongly and be centrally aligned where appropriate (e.g., Availability Confirmation). They currently feel weak.
  - Standardise cards, tables, badges, inputs, and buttons across all candidate + school pages.

     - Add/centralize CSS variables in one place (e.g., :root or a theme file) for:
       --bg, --panel, --panel-2, --border, --text, --muted, --accent-red, --accent-red-hover, --shadow, --radius, --focus
     - Ensure candidate + school use the exact same tokens as admin.

     - Background: near-black / deep charcoal (not maroon).
     - Border: subtle 1px border with low-alpha light edge.
     - Shadows: minimal, crisp (avoid heavy glows).
     - Radius: consistent (e.g., 14–18px).
     - Headings: slightly larger, clearer weight, less grey.

     - Define primary / secondary / ghost button styles once.

› You are working in the CoverMeNow ONE portal codebase.

  TASK: Refactor the Candidate Profile screen so it matches the darker “admin dashboard” look, uses full-width layout for profile details, and ONLY shows editable form fields when the user explicitly enters
  edit mode (via a pencil / Edit button).

  IMPORTANT BEHAVIOUR CHANGE (DO THIS FIRST):
  - Default view = READ-ONLY “Profile Summary” cards only (no input fields visible).
  - Clicking the pencil/Edit button toggles EDIT MODE for that section (or the whole page if that’s how it’s structured).
  - In EDIT MODE: show the form inputs and Save/Cancel controls.
  - Save persists changes and returns to READ-ONLY view.
  - Cancel discards changes and returns to READ-ONLY view.

  UI / LAYOUT REQUIREMENTS:
  1) Full-width layout (no narrow stacked feel)
  - On desktop: use a single, full-width container for the profile content (max-width can remain, but the cards should span the available content width, not cramped two-up columns).
  - Convert “Personal Details” + “Role & Preferences” into a full-width layout:
    - Option A (preferred): a single full-width “Profile Summary” card with a clean grid inside (2–4 columns depending on space) showing key/value “chips”/rows.
    - Option B: keep two cards, but make them full-width stacked sections with generous internal grids (not two columns side-by-side).

  2) Read-only presentation (default)
  - Replace the current mixed read-only + inputs with:
    - A “Personal Details” summary section (name, email, phone, nationality, address)
    - A “Role & Preferences” summary section (primary role, other role, travel radius, location, driving licence, own vehicle, QTS, DBS held, update service, availability days)
  - Each section has a clear Edit (pencil) control on the right of the section header.

  3) Edit presentation (only when editing)
  - When editing a section:
    - Show the relevant inputs in a clean 2-column (or 3-column) grid where appropriate.
    - Keep labels compact.
    - Keep input heights consistent.
    - Show Save / Cancel near the section header (right aligned), NOT floating over the page and NOT duplicated at multiple scroll positions.
  - If you currently have a global Save/Cancel bar, remove it or only show it in edit mode and keep it pinned INSIDE the section/card.

  4) Visual facelift: match admin “more black”
  - Reduce the red/burgundy haze. Make backgrounds closer to charcoal/near-black.
  - Cards: subtle dark panels, crisp borders (low-opacity), minimal glow.
  - Keep red as accent only (buttons, active states), not as a broad background wash.
  - Increase perceived sharpness:
    - Slightly stronger border contrast on cards/inputs
    - Slightly higher text contrast
    - Reduce blur intensity on the big background gradient
    - Ensure consistent padding/radius across cards

     - Add edit state handling:
       - If PHP-rendered: use a simple JS toggle + CSS classes.
       - If React/JS: component state per section: `isEditingPersonal`, `isEditingRole`.
  C) Buttons:
     - Add pencil icon (or “Edit” button) in each section header.
       - `--panel-bg`, `--panel-border`, `--text-primary`, `--text-muted`, `--accent-red`
     - Apply a consistent card style used in admin dashboard (reuse admin classes if they exist).
     - Update the profile page background overlay to be darker/cleaner.

  ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA:
  - Clicking Cancel returns to summary view and discards unsaved changes.
  - Clicking Save persists and returns to summary view.
  - Styling is darker/cleaner like admin (less red haze), with crisp panels and consistent spacing.

  DELIVERABLES:
  - Commit/patch the updated template/component + CSS.
  - If you create new CSS classes, keep them scoped (e.g. `.cmn-profile ...`) and avoid breaking other pages.

  Now implement it.

› CODEX PROMPT (copy/paste)

  Goal: Refactor the Candidate Profile screen UI/UX to match the newer “admin dashboard” facelift: darker/blacker, cleaner, less stacked, more breathing room, full-width sections. Most importantly: STOP
  rendering editable form fields by default. The page should be READ-ONLY by default and only show input fields when the user explicitly clicks an Edit (pencil) control.

  Scope: Candidate dashboard → Profile page (candidate-profile).
  - Read-only “view mode” shows all profile info in a clean, full-width layout.
  - “Edit mode” (triggered by pencil) replaces the read-only display with an actual form (inputs/selects/checkboxes).
  - No duplicated information. Do NOT show read-only values AND inputs at the same time.
  - If edit is cancelled, revert to view mode without changing data.
  - If saved, persist and return to view mode.

  Layout requirements (VIEW MODE):
  1) Use a full-width single-column layout for the main content area (no 2-column split for Personal vs Role). The sections should stretch across the content container width.
  2) Break content into 3–5 full-width cards/sections, with a consistent “admin” style:
     - Personal Details (name, email, phone, nationality)
     - Address (house/number, address lines, town/city, county, postcode, country)
     - Role & Preferences (primary role, other role, travel radius, location, driving licence, own vehicle, QTS, DBS held, DBS update service, availability days)
     - Notes (full-width block)
     - Documents (status + list)
  3) Within each section, display fields in a “definition list / key-value grid” style:
     - Labels on the left, values on the right (or 2–3 column grid of key-value pairs depending on width).
     - Avoid big empty space; show more information density but keep it clean.
  4) Each section has a small pencil icon button in the top-right (“Edit”) OR one global “Edit Profile” button at the top-right that toggles all sections. Prefer global Edit for simplicity.

  Layout requirements (EDIT MODE):
  1) Replace the entire page content with a structured form (same sections), using full-width rows with 2–3 columns where appropriate.
  2) Buttons:
     - Primary: “Save changes”
     - Secondary: “Cancel”
  4) Validation + UX:
     - Keep existing validation rules.
     - Highlight invalid fields with subtle red border + helper text.
  - Keep the left sidebar as-is; only refactor the main content area.

  Technical requirements:
  - Keep existing backend endpoints and data model intact.
  - Introduce a local UI state: isEditing (boolean).
  - View mode uses the existing fetched data and renders it.
  - Edit mode initializes form state from the fetched data (single source of truth).
  - On save success: update local displayed data and exit edit mode.
  - On cancel: discard form state and exit edit mode.
  - Make sure the “Edit” control does not appear twice or per-section if you choose global edit.

  Deliverables:
  1) Implement view-mode UI (full-width sections + key-value layout).
  2) Implement edit-mode toggle, edit form, Save/Cancel flow.
  3) Apply darker/cleaner styling consistent with admin dashboard.
  4) Ensure responsiveness (works on laptop widths without feeling stacked).

  Do not:
  - Do not reuse old prompts or previous layout patterns.
  - Do not show duplicated info (read-only + inputs at once).
  - Do not keep the current stacked edit boxes visible in view mode.

  Now implement this refactor in the existing candidate profile page and associated CSS/components.


• I’m now implementing this as a true global view/edit profile flow: read-only full-width sections by default, one Edit Profile control, and one unified edit form with sticky Save/Cancel. I’ll patch PHP markup
  first, then wire JS and CSS.

• Implemented and deployed.

  ### What changed

  - wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/covermenowone-one.php
      - Candidate Profile now has a single global edit control in the profile header (Edit Profile ✎).
      - Added a global Save/Cancel bar for edit mode (no per-section duplicate edit buttons).
          - Address
          - Role & Preferences
          - Notes
          - Documents
      - Added read-only field targets for address/notes so they update immediately after save.
  - wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/frontend.js
      - Replaced section-by-section edit logic with a global isEditing flow:
          - View mode (default): read-only values only.
          - Edit mode: inputs only (no duplicate read-only content).
      - Save/Cancel behavior:
          - Save changes submits once, disables while saving, updates local UI, exits edit mode.
          - Cancel resets form state and exits edit mode without persisting.
      - On save success, updates all summary fields (including address and notes) and profile completion/gate state.
  - wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/frontend.css
      - Added styling for global profile action bar and cleaner admin-like darker profile layout.
      - Enforced full-width profile cards and consistent spacing.
      - Hid legacy per-section action rows and aligned profile header actions.
      - Kept dark/glass style but with cleaner, sharper surfaces.

  ### Result

  - Candidate Profile is now read-only by default.
  - Inputs show only after explicit Edit.
  - No mixed read-only + input duplication.
  - Save/Cancel flow behaves as requested and returns to read-only.

  ### Deploy

  - Deployed via SKIP_MARKETING_DEPLOY=1 ./deploy_portal.sh.
  - Syntax checks passed:
      - php -l wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/covermenowone-one.php
      - node --check wp-plugin/covermenowone-one/frontend.js

  If you want, I can now do a second pass to tighten the edit form density (3-column on very wide desktop) without changing behavior.
